# run9 英語レビュー: ワールド17・18（レッスン 481〜540）

- 日付: 2026-11-27
- やりかた: docs/run9/BRIEF.md の「Review method」。lint → 3人の レビュアー（A=アメリカの 小学校の 先生 / B=こどもの ESL・言語習得の 専門家 / C=台本を 読む 声優）を べつべつに 書いて から 決める。2人 いじょうが 指摘 or 客観的な まちがい（objective）だけ なおす。10 レッスンずつ 6 グループで レビュー
- lint（`npm run english:lint -- --from 481 --to 540`）: **まえ error 0 / warn 86 / info 17 → あと error 0 / warn 1 / info 17**（まえの warn: Okay・okay 77・Please enjoy 4・That's all for today 2・ながい 文 2・key chain と keychain の ゆれ 1。あとの warn 1: L485 の 会話「Then, we're going to put on our jackets and go to the roof.」〈13 語〉。First / Then / After that / Finally の 4つの じゅんばんの 2つめで、しぜんな 英語なので のこす）
- なおした 欄: **616**（パッチ 649 件。機械的な なおし 15・ブロックの きめごと 342〈340 欄〉・レビュアー 231・あとの そろえ 57・lint の そろえ 4）。なおした レッスン: **60 / 60**
- 60 レッスン ぜんぶに `"reviewed": "run9"` を つけた
- パッチは ぜんぶ「before が いまの 値と おなじ ときだけ あてる」で あてた（はずれ 0）。60 レッスン ぜんぶで スライドの 検証（unknownSlideItems）・台本の 長さ（180〜240 秒）・英語の わりあいを たしかめた（ALL OK）。かいわ あいての チェック（.run9-w1718/partner.ts）も ALL OK

## 共通の きめごと（ブロック全体）
1. 日本語の なかの 英語の あとの 2重の 記号（「How about you?。」）→ 英語がわの 記号だけ のこす（W01-16 と おなじ）
2. 話しことばの 2択「Yes, I am. Or, No, I'm not.」→「Yes, I am. Or no, I'm not.」、英語字幕は「A / B」（W03-16 と おなじ）。ただし L514・L515・L523 の「A. Or, b ...」「Walk... or Speak...」は 2択の クイズでは なく ひとつづきの 文なので、字幕も「Or a cat ...」「Walk... or speak...」に した（道具の けっかを 手で なおした: mechfix.mjs）
3. 「Okay / okay」→「OK」（スタイルガイドの 決まった 形。lint の 77 warn。teacherScript・会話・スライド・L484・L523 の 学校の 表現も）
4. 文の なかの「Now, Ring ～」「Then, Look ～」「After that, Watch ～」「Finally, Drink ～」→ 小文字（say・英語字幕・teacherScript。TPR の 項目と スライドは 大文字の まま。名前と I'm は そのまま）。L485 の「First, Then, After that, Finally.」も「First, then, after that, finally.」
5. イギリスふうの ほめことば: L485「Brilliant planning!」→「Great planning!」、L493・L504「Brilliant!」→「Fantastic!」、L516「Lovely work, everyone!」→「Great work, everyone!」、L523「Lovely work, quiet explorers!」→「Nice work, quiet explorers!」、L516・L538 の teacherScript「Lovely!」→「Great!」、L502 会話「What a lovely name.」→「What a beautiful name.」
6. エマ先生 じぶんの 語りの「Ms. Emma says, / asks, / smiles.」→「I say, / I ask, / I smile.」、日本語「エマ先生「」→「わたし「」（L503・L505・L508・L509・L510。L490 の「And I say, Bravo!」／「わたし「ブラボー！」」と そろえた。W15-16 の 報告より）
7. L510「I think it will be a wonderful show. Please enjoy!」→「… Have fun!」（Please enjoy! は「たのしんで ください」の 直訳で 英語では いわない。lint stiff-phrase。学校の 表現・say・字幕・スライド。日本語は そのまま）
8. L490 の さいごの あいさつ「That's all for today. See you!」→「That's it for today. See you!」（lint stiff-phrase）。ニュースの きまり文句「That's all for today's weather.」は 学習項目で、ほんものの 天気よほうの いいかた なので のこした
9. L510 の teacherScript「speed TPR」→「speed challenge」（W13-16 と おなじ）
10. あとの そろえ（グループの 報告から）:
    - **AI かいわの さいしょの ひとこと「Hi! I'm Ms. Emma.」→ ほんとうの かいわ あいて**（L489・L500・L501・L511〜540 の 33 レッスン）。アプリは かいわ あいてに エマ先生を えらばない（src/lib/conversation/engine.ts の pickConversationPartner）ので、ハルくんたちが「I'm Ms. Emma.」と いって しまって いた。さいごの「Next, talk with X!」の X に した（L501-510 の グループの 報告。partner.ts で アプリと おなじ 関数を つかって たしかめた）
    - L504 の かいわ あいては ビリモ なのに、ひとことが ゲストの「Hi! I'm Luka.」だった（ルカは アプリの かいわ あいてに なれない）→「Hi! I'm Birimo. Let's make a promise, like Luka! I'll call you tomorrow. What time is good for you?」、goalJa「ルカと」→「ビリモと」
    - L483「key chain」→「keychain」（L528・ワールド16 L459 と そろえた。lint inconsistent-form。アメリカで ふつうの かきかた）
    - TURE の 音の かきかた「cher」→「chur」（L506・L508・L510。TTS が 名前の Cher と よむ。L488・L489 は グループで chur に した）
    - L481・L484 の 音節の よみ: グループが「Ob, ser, vay, shun」「Ih, ma, jih, nay, shun」と 音で かいたが、lint の spelling error に なり 字幕にも 出るので、この ブロックの「DI-no-saur」「Vol-CA-no」と おなじ ハイフンの かたち「Ob-ser-va-tion」「Im-ag-i-na-tion」に した

## 学習項目 ID が かわった もの（記録が あたらしく なる）
- L483 ことば「key chain」→「keychain」
- L483 表現「I'm going to give her this key chain.」→「I'm going to give her this keychain.」
- L484 表現「Okay. Maybe next time!」→「OK. Maybe next time!」
- L510 表現「I think it will be a wonderful show. Please enjoy!」→「I think it will be a wonderful show. Have fun!」
- L523 表現「Speak softly, please. ― Okay.」→「Speak softly, please. ― OK.」

## のこした こと（1人だけの 指摘・ほかの ワールドにも ある・あとで 決める）
- L485 の 13 語の 会話の 文（lint warn 1）
- 「Well done!」（L504・L530・L532）は アメリカでも ふつう
- 「That's all for today's weather.」（L490 の 学習項目。天気よほうの きまり文句）
- 「zhun」（-sion の 音。L486・L490・L495・L506）: へんだが ブロックで そろって いるので のこした。TTS の よみかたの ヒントで 決める
- L488「fifteen degrees」（せっし。アメリカの こどもには さむく きこえるが、日本の こども むけ なので のこした）
- phonics.tipJa の「ブロック1 の まとめ:」（L485・L490・L495・L505）: 1人の 指摘。まちがいでは ないので のこした
- 学習項目: L522 TPR「Get ready, set, go!」（ふつうは Ready, set, go!）、L533「untidy」（ふつうは messy）。まちがいでは なく ID が かわるので のこした
- 「Valley Museum / valley museum」「Valley Zoo / valley zoo」の 大文字の ゆれ（L514・L517）、teacherScript「l, y」と スライド「L Y」の ゆれ（L521〜524）
- まとめの「You can say X and Y」の ことばを ならべる 文（L522・L526・L528・L529）: よめるので のこした（ながすぎる ものは グループで 2つの 文に わけた）
- わざと まちがえた かたち（beautifuler・colorfuler・more far の クイズ）、「very, very」（lint info）
- ほかの ワールドで みつけた もの: AI かいわの「Hi! I'm Ms. Emma.」（W19 いこうも あるか しらべる）、まとめの「, and you can ～」で 12 語を こえる 文

## レビュアーの メモ（グループごと・英語）

### Run 9 review notes: L481-490 (world 17, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I am. Or no, I'm not." says, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First, / After that, / Finally,", L490 seg20 "That's it for today.", L483 "key chain" -> "keychain"). There are 24 patches in 9 lessons. L487 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this world is already clean and natural, so the fixes are small. Themes: (1) TTS problems in syllable and sound spell-outs ("Ob, ser, va, tion", "I, ma, gi, na, tion", "cher" read like the name Cher); (2) wording a teacher would not use ("star goods", "a big paper", "That uneasy feeling about later", "A what question needs a plan", "feel safe" for a worried friend); (3) punctuation that breaks the reading (a question mark mid-sentence, a stray comma, capitals mid-sentence, a sentence starting with lowercase "t, i, o, n", a fragment with no verb); (4) one UK spelling ("night-time"). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 210-227 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L481 The Observatory on the Hill
- A: The arrival scene is natural ("Look! There's a white building on the hill!", "We found it!"). seg1 "When you already made a plan, use going to." is normal American usage (simple past with already), so I kept it.
- B: going to for plans is introduced clearly, and the "No S, no E D" rule fits the quiz. In seg10, the syllable model "Ob, ser, va, tion" gives the wrong vowel (/vɑ/ instead of /veɪ/) and spells the ending tion instead of the sound shun that the lesson teaches. The JA ヴェイ・ション is already right.
- C: TTS will read "va" as "vah" and "tion" as "tee-on". The rest reads well.
- Decision: seg10 say/subtitleEn "Ob, ser, va, tion." -> "Ob, ser, vay, shun." (B+C).

#### L482 What Are You Going to Do?
- A: The cafeteria scene and the "game of catch" image for How about you? are natural. In seg18, "A what question needs a plan, not yes or no." is not how a teacher says it. The question doesn't need a plan. The answer is a plan.
- B: The yes/no distractor is a good check. The teacherScript says "Today, we have a day off. So let's talk about plans for the weekend.", which is a small logic jump (single flag, kept).
- C: In seg18, "It's A! A what question..." stumbles on the double "A".
- Decision: seg18 say/subtitleEn -> "It's A! For a what question, answer with your plan, not yes or no." (A+C). The JA already says よていで こたえるよ.

#### L483 I'm Going to Visit My Grandma
- A: The gift-shop scene and Haru's "He loves the moon." are warm. The teacherScript says "Look at all the star goods!", but an American teacher would not say "goods" to kids. It comes from グッズ. Birimo's "a planet sticker for me" would be "for myself" from an adult, but it is fine and funny from Birimo (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "star goods" (calque). This is for + person is clear. JA おとうと for "brother" is OK.
- C: Reads well. "Ding-a-ling!" and "Jingle, jingle." are easy to say.
- Decision: teacherScript "star goods" -> "star gifts" (A+B). I did not touch "key chain", because the keychain change is block-wide.

#### L484 Are You Going to Join Us?
- A: The invitation scene and the hot-cocoa twist are natural. In the teacherScript, "If you say no, tell your plan." needs a listener ("tell them your plan"). In seg4, "A big paper on the wall" uses paper as a countable noun. seg2 "Me too!" as a picture of "join" is a little odd but harmless (single flag, kept).
- B: The kind-refusal pattern (No, I'm not. + plan + Maybe next time!) is good. In seg12, "For Yes, I am, Nod your head. For No, I'm not, Shake your head." has capitals mid-sentence. In the teacherScript, "Long words, but the end is easy. t, i, o, n." starts a sentence with a lowercase letter.
- C: In seg10, the clap model "I, ma, gi, na, tion" will be read as "eye, ma, ghee, nah, tee-on".
- Decision: teacherScript "tell your plan" -> "tell them your plan" and "t, i, o, n." -> "It's t, i, o, n." (A+B). seg4 -> "A big sheet of paper on the wall." (objective). seg10 -> "Ih, ma, jih, nay, shun." to match JA イ・マ・ジ・ネイ・ション (B+C). seg12 "Nod / Shake" -> "nod / shake" (objective).

#### L485 Our Plan for Tonight
- A: The four-step plan with First / Then / After that / Finally is natural, and so is Birimo's "What time?" follow-up. conversation.scene "night-time" is British spelling.
- B: The link back to World 16's order words is clear. The "stairs" image for the four steps is good. phonics.tipJa starts with the internal label "ブロック1 の まとめ:" (block-wide note, kept).
- C: Reads well. "Step, step, step, step!" is easy to say.
- Decision: conversation.scene "night-time" -> "nighttime" (objective, US spelling).

#### L486 Will It Rain Tomorrow?
- A: The weather-room scene and the Kiko/Birimo disagreement are natural. The going to vs will contrast in seg1 is the one teachers use. In seg20, "You can ask Will it rain tomorrow? and make a guess." puts a question mark in the middle of a sentence.
- B: "I think it will. / I don't think so." is well chosen for a guess. The -sion /ʒən/ words are correct.
- C: The mid-sentence "?" makes TTS stop with a rising tone and then restart on a lowercase "and". "Zhun." is an odd spelling, but it is used the same way in every segment (kept).
- Decision: seg20 say/subtitleEn -> "You can ask, Will it rain tomorrow? And you can make a guess." (A+C).

#### L487 Tomorrow's Weather
- A: The reporter role-play is natural ("Here is tomorrow's weather.", "Don't forget your umbrella!", "You sound like real reporters!"). The "Good evening" show opener in a morning lesson is fine as role-play.
- B: The It will be + weather word / It will + verb split is clear, and the "rains" distractor is good. -ssion as /ʃən/ is correct.
- C: Reads well. "Lights, camera, smile!" is fun to say.
- Decision: no change.

#### L488 It Won't Be Cold
- A: The worried-Kiko scene is warm. seg1 "Help a friend feel safe." and seg18 "You help friends feel safe." use "safe", which is about danger. For a worry, a teacher says "feel better", and Kiko says "I feel better now." herself. seg5 "That uneasy feeling about later." is not natural. "It's fifteen degrees." would sound freezing to an American (Fahrenheit), but Japanese kids think in Celsius, so it is a single flag and kept.
- B: Same flags on "feel safe" (JA あんしん is "relieved") and on seg5 ("uneasy" is above level). The teacherScript says "cher", but seg9 says "chur".
- C: In seg8, "just in case means, to be safe" has a stray comma that breaks the phrase. "cher" will likely be read like the name Cher (/ʃɛr/).
- Decision: seg1 and seg18 "feel safe" -> "feel better" (A+B). seg5 -> "Worry. You keep thinking about what might happen. Your tummy feels funny. Worry!" (A+B). seg8 comma removed (A+C). teacherScript "cher" -> "chur" (B+C). The JA is unchanged because it already matches.

#### L489 I'll Help You
- A: The carry-the-telescope scene is natural ("I'll get a flashlight.", "And I'll carry the blankets!"). The "next week is way too late" quiz joke works.
- B: I'll for on-the-spot decisions vs going to for plans is clear. The teacherScript has the same "cher" vs "chur" mismatch as L488.
- C: "cher" is the same TTS risk. "Slowly, but fast? Ha ha!" in the speed challenge is a playful, readable joke.
- Decision: teacherScript "cher" -> "chur" (B+C).

#### L490 Weather Report Show
- A: The show finale is natural, and the reporter sign-off is a known keeper. In the teacherScript, "And at the end, That's all for today's weather. Have a nice day!" has no verb, so it sounds like Ms. Emma is ending the lesson in the middle.
- B: The tion / sion / ssion / ture review in seg9 is clear. seg19 "and That's all ends it" uses the quoted-phrase capital style the product uses everywhere (kept). The phonics.tipJa "ブロック2 の まとめ:" label is the same as in L485.
- C: Same flag on the missing verb. The listener can't tell where the model sentence starts.
- Decision: teacherScript "And at the end, That's all..." -> "And at the end, say, That's all..." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Syllable spell-outs written as letters, not sounds** (L481 seg10, L484 seg10): TTS reads "tion" as "tee-on", "I" as "eye" and "va" as "vah". I fixed them here with sound spellings ("vay, shun", "Ih, ma, jih, nay, shun"). Scan the other groups for ", tion." and clap-the-beats lines.
- **"cher" vs "chur" for -ture**: the teacherScripts in L488 and L489 used "cher" (fixed), and the segments use "chur". Check L491+ teacherScripts for "cher".
- **"Zhun" for /ʒən/** (L486, L490): odd but consistent, so it is kept. If TTS misreads it, change all instances together.
- **"fifteen degrees"** (L488 conversation, seg6, seg11): this is Celsius, which an American would hear as freezing. It is kept because the learners are Japanese. If the owner wants strict US usage, change it to "It's sixty degrees." with JA 15ど kept, or add "Celsius". This is a learning-adjacent conversation line, so it is not changed here.
- **phonics.tipJa "ブロック1 の まとめ:" / "ブロック2 の まとめ:"** (L485, L490): an internal planning label with an ASCII colon, shown in a kid-facing tip. Consider "ふりかえり:" or dropping it. Not changed here.
- **Quoted-phrase capitals mid-sentence** ("say It will be sunny and It will rain", "That's all ends it"): this is the product-wide convention for quoted model sentences, so it is kept. A plain imperative after a comma ("For Yes, I am, Nod your head") is not a quote and was fixed (L484).
- Kept on purpose: L481 "When you already made a plan"; L482 "Today, we have a day off."; L483 Birimo's "a planet sticker for me"; L484 seg2 "Me too!"; L484 Haru's "OK, I'm going to join you!" (target form); L487 "Good evening!" role-play in a morning lesson; L489 "Slowly, but fast? Ha ha!"

### Run 9 review notes: L491-500 (world 17, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I am. Or no, I'm not." says, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,", "speed challenge"). There are 37 patches in 9 lessons. L499 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. One conversation line changed (L497 Birimo "cook star cookies" -> "bake star cookies"), with its seg7 say/subtitleEn/slide item; it is not a learning item and tests/ has no reference to it. The English here is clean and on level, so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) clipped note-style English a teacher would not say aloud ("so goes.", "so no will", "Will not gets short.", "Two short families", "we will made short"); (2) stacked wrap-up lines that run a sentence and bare words together (L492 seg20, L495 seg20); (3) speaker or order slips in read-aloud dialog (L495 seg6 missing "I say," before Ms. Emma's reply, L498 teacherScript practice in the wrong order, L491 "where W I ran away"); (4) one count slip ("Listen to two sentences" before four); (5) EN/JA mismatch in L497 ("I'll be a doctor." with JA ～と おもう) and the quiz master saying "I think it's..." for the answer (JA こたえは…); (6) one capital mid-sentence ("Look down and Wave"). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 209-226 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L491 The Moon and the Earth
- A: The planetarium scene and Birimo's "So one trip is one birthday!" are natural and fun. The facts are right (about one month, about one year). seg19 "A fact is always true, so goes." stops short; seg1 already says "so we say goes".
- B: The facts-use-present rule is clear and matches the grammar note. The 'll tipJa (' は きえた wi) and seg9 JA (w と i が にげた) agree. Same flag on seg19.
- C: seg9 "The little mark shows where W I ran away." is heard as "W... I ran away". seg19 "so goes." sounds unfinished.
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn "where W I ran away" -> "where W and I ran away" (A+C). seg19 say/subtitleEn "so goes." -> "so we say goes." (A+C).

#### L492 Planets in Space
- A: The model table and Birimo's sister line are natural. The teacherScript says "Listen to two sentences." and then gives four. seg20 "You can say Saturn has rings, and she'll and it'll." joins a sentence and two bare words.
- B: The is (color, size) / has (parts) contrast is clear. seg1 "has for something it owns" is a little odd for a planet (single flag, kept). Same flag on seg20.
- C: seg20 is hard to phrase aloud. The rest reads well ("Swoosh!", "Out of this world!").
- Decision: teacherScript "Listen to two sentences." -> "Listen to these sentences." (objective, count). seg20 say/subtitleEn split into "You can say Saturn has rings. And you can say she'll and it'll." (A+C).

#### L493 Life on a Space Station
- A: The exhibit, the wet towels and Riko's "No bath? I couldn't live there!" are natural and correct. seg8 "so no will" is note style. seg18 "Will not gets short." is not something a teacher says, and it is not a question before "Is it A...?".
- B: The everyday-present point and the won't special case are clear. Same flag on seg18 (JA asks みじかく すると？). seg0 "Everything is floating. Even my hair!" in a model room is pretend play (single flag, kept).
- C: seg8 "so no will" reads choppy. "Not willn't." is fine as a contrast.
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "so we don't need will" (A+C; JA will は いらない). seg18 say/subtitleEn -> "What's the short way to say will not?" (A+B).

#### L494 Shooting Stars
- A: The roof scene and "It's a secret! I won't tell you." are natural. seg9 "Two short families." says the families are short. seg0 "Blankets on the floor" on a roof is slightly odd (single flag, kept).
- B: The 'll / n't sorting is a good review. Same flag on seg9; the JA is みじかい かたちの ふたつの なかま.
- C: Reads well ("Wait for it... wait for it...", "Shh, a quiet wish."). seg0 JA こんにちは at night (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn "Two short families." -> "Two families of short forms." (A+B).

#### L495 Space Quiz Night
- A: The quiz-show frame is natural ("Ding ding!", "One point for the red team."). seg1 "Ask a quiz with which" (you ask a quiz question). seg19 has the quiz master say "I think it's... B, Jupiter!", but she knows the answer. seg20 is a 15-word list with "say Good try! kindly".
- B: The kind-feedback point (Good try!) is good. seg19 EN does not match JA こたえは…. Same flag on seg1.
- C: In seg6, "I think it's Jupiter! That's right!" has no speaker change, so "That's right!" sounds like Haru. seg20 cannot be read clearly. seg9 "zhun" for -sion may be read oddly by TTS (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg1 say/subtitleEn "a quiz" -> "a quiz question" (A+B). seg6 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa add "I say," / わたし before "That's right!" (A+C). seg19 say/subtitleEn "I think it's..." -> "The answer is..." (A+B). seg20 say/subtitleEn -> "You can ask, Which planet is...? You can answer, I think it's... And you can be kind and say, Good try!" (A+C).

#### L496 Someday I Will Fly
- A: The photo hall and "Your dream doesn't have to be about space." are natural and kind. teacherScript "See is with your eyes." is not how a teacher explains it. seg19 "ready for blast off" (noun "blastoff") is a spelling nit (single flag, kept).
- B: "After will, no S" and the homophone pairs are clear. Same flag on the teacherScript line. Kiko "draw picture books" is OK for an illustrator (kept).
- C: seg12 "Look down and Wave to the Earth." has a capital mid-sentence. The rest reads well.
- Decision: teacherScript "See is with your eyes." -> "You see with your eyes." (A+B). seg12 say/subtitleEn "and Wave" -> "and wave" (objective).

#### L497 What Will You Be?
- A: The twenty-years game is fun. Birimo "I'll cook star cookies" is odd (you bake cookies; L481 already says "bake star cookies"). teacherScript "We aren't sure, so we can say" states it as a fact. seg10 "Our team won one point!" is a deliberate sound line (kept).
- B: school.expressions[1] "I'll be a doctor." has JA おいしゃさんに なっていると おもう, which adds "I think" (seg15 subtitleJa too). seg8 English ends on "Not sure yet?" and the answer is only in Japanese. Same flag on the teacherScript.
- C: "cook star cookies" is a small sound clash aloud. seg8 subtitleEn stops on a question.
- Decision: school.expressions[1].ja and seg15 subtitleJa -> おいしゃさんに なっているよ。 (objective, JA). teacherScript -> "Not sure? Then you can say, I think I'll be a teacher." (A+B). conversation.lines[4] and seg7 say/subtitleEn/slide.items[1] "cook" -> "bake" (A+C). seg8 say/subtitleEn add "That's OK!" (A+B).

#### L498 Robots Will Help Us
- A: The Future Life room and Birimo's "The sky will be too busy! Birds won't like it." are natural. The teacherScript practice goes "I think robots will cook dinner. Really? I don't think so. Why?", which is out of order.
- B: Same flag: it should model the taught pattern (I don't think so. -> Really? Why?). The two/to/too and blue/blew examples are correct.
- C: The practice line is confusing to voice, because one speaker seems to say "Really?" and "I don't think so" together.
- Decision: teacherScript practice -> "I think robots will cook dinner. I don't think so. Really? Why? Robots can't taste food!" (A+B+C).

#### L499 Because It's Exciting
- A: The rocket trip and Birimo's "Because it's close. I can come home for dinner!" are natural.
- B: Because for reasons and the because/but quiz are clear. EN and JA match. seg1 "When someone asks Why? answer with Because." has odd punctuation, but it reads clearly (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well ("Boing... slowly... boing!", "Can you hear me? I'm right here!").
- Decision: no change.

#### L500 Lesson 500 Star Party
- A: The 500th party, the Lesson 1 callback and "I'm so proud of you." are warm and natural. "We studied together for five hundred lessons." is OK in US English (present perfect would be beyond Stage 2; kept). "There are seven hundred more lessons." matches 40 worlds x 30 lessons.
- B: The Let's / We will / Congratulations-Thank you points are clear. seg9 "And we'll, we will made short." is hard to parse.
- C: Same flag on seg9 when read aloud.
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn -> "And we'll is short for we will." (A+C; JA already says this).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Clipped "so + word" rule lines** ("so goes.", "so no will"): fixed in L491 seg19 and L493 seg8. The same terse Answer/Point template may appear in other lessons ("so has.", "so is."). Scan for ", so [a-z]+\." in say.
- **Wrap-up lines that stack a sentence with bare words** ("You can say X, and she'll and it'll", "ask which planet, answer with I think it's, and say ...") : fixed in L492 and L495. Check other seg20 lines for 13+ word "You can ..." lists.
- **Unmarked speaker change in "Let's listen" segments**: L495 seg6 had no "I say," before Ms. Emma's reply. Scan seg6/seg7 lines for a reply that directly follows another character's line.
- **Homophone and short-form teacherScripts**: "See is with your eyes" (L496) was the only odd one in this group. L497-L499 are fine.
- **TTS for "zhun"** (L495 seg9, -sion): may be read oddly. Kept. If the TTS has a lexicon, add a hint.
- **Night greetings**: L494 seg0 "Hi, everyone!" / こんにちは on the night roof, while the teacherScript says "Good evening". Kept (single flag).
- Kept on purpose: L492 seg1 "has for something it owns"; L493 seg0 "Everything is floating" in the model room; L494 seg0 "Blankets on the floor"; L496 "ready for blast off" and "draw picture books"; L497 seg10 "Our team won one point!"; L499 seg1 punctuation; L500 "We studied together for five hundred lessons."; "―" in pattern fields and slide items.

### Run 9 review notes: L501-510 (world 17, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, testPoints, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,", L510 "Have fun!", L510 "speed challenge"). There are 44 patches in 10 lessons. Every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this world is warm and natural (the star story, the Happy Cat, Luka, the time capsule), so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) the "Here's the point" grammar segments read the rule as comma fragments ("We live on, for our home.", "What are you going to, for any plan.", "A plan you already decided, going to.") in L503, L506, L508 and L509. I gave each one a verb ("Use ~ for ~."). The same problem, a stray comma in "Why don't we, is", was in L507. (2) In the retold dialogue, a line had no speaker, so it sounded like the wrong character (L506 "Wow, you got a line!", L507 "Of course!"). (3) Wording a teacher would not use ("a paper full of dots", "Riko, please start.", "Show your plans and dreams", "passes the talk", "Are you, so answer"). (4) Unnatural Japanese (うみや もりや どうぶつが あります/います, ほんの すこしの ぎょう). (5) "Yesterday" in L502, changed to "Last time" as in W13-16. Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 215-227 s long with 97-99% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L501 Night Sky Story
- A: The bedtime-style star story is lovely ("Come up and sleep with us!", "Sweet dreams!"). In seg20, "a long ago story" needs a hyphen because it comes before a noun. "Many, many years before you and me" is casual but fine.
- B: The past-form point (see/saw, call/called) and the present/past quiz fit the lesson. The JA すぎた かたち follows the central decision. The aiTask starter is Ms. Emma, but seg20 says "Next, talk with Haru!" (see block-wide notes; not changed).
- C: Reads softly and well. "North. Storm. Orbit. Short." is easy to say.
- Decision: seg20 say/subtitleEn "a long ago story" -> "a long-ago story" (objective, hyphen).

#### L502 My Own Constellation
- A: Making and naming constellations is natural, and "Same stars, different pictures!" is a nice line. seg0 "Grab a paper full of dots" is wrong, because "a paper" means a newspaper or an essay. The teacherScript starts with "Yesterday", which may not be true.
- B: The look like / looks like point and the "It look a guitar" distractor work. seg1 "give them a name. Let's call it ..." switches from them to it (single flag, kept). The seg19 answer slide shows Riko's "I think they look like a guitar." It is valid, so it stays.
- C: Reads well. "More. Shore. Before. Explore." is easy to say.
- Decision: teacherScript "Yesterday" -> "Last time" (A+B, same as W13-16). seg0 "a paper full of dots" -> "a page full of dots" (objective).

#### L503 A Message to Space
- A: The space message and the privacy rule (no real name or address, sign "From the children of Earth") are good. seg8 "We live on, for our home. We like, for fun things." is not how a teacher explains this.
- B: The JA うみや もりや たくさんの どうぶつが あります (expressions[1], seg15) is wrong for animals. The conversation version, ... どうぶつが います (lines[3], seg6), is wrong for うみ and もり. Same flag on seg8.
- C: The seg8 comma fragments trip the reader. "The rocket goes roar! All aboard!" is fun to say.
- Decision: expressions[1].ja, seg15 subtitleJa, conversation.lines[3].ja and seg6 subtitleJa -> うみや もりが あって、どうぶつも たくさん います。 (objective, JA). seg8 -> "Use We live on for our home. Use We like for fun things. Use We have for things on Earth." (A+C).

#### L504 I'll Call You Tomorrow
- A: Birimo playing Luka works, and the promise talk is natural ("Pinky promise.", "See you tomorrow on the screen!"). The teacherScript says Luka "wants to talk with us again" before we have met him. Kiko's "Thank you, Luka." after "Thank you for coming!" is a little flat (single flag, kept).
- B: The same flag on "again" (seg0 says he wants to be our friend). The online-safety note is good. The note that hour and our sound different is helpful.
- C: Reads well. "Four. Pour. Your. Fourteen." is clean.
- Decision: teacherScript "talk with us again" -> "talk with us" (A+B).

#### L505 Future Fair
- A: The fair, the booth and Riko's speech are realistic. "Riko, please start." from the teacher sounds stiff and cold. "Riko, would you like to start?" is what a teacher says. "A good speaker looks at the people" could be "looks at the audience" (single flag, kept).
- B: The style guide flags Please + verb from teacher to child. The or/ore/oar/our review is clear. The tipJa "ブロック5 の まとめ" uses an internal word (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well. The long quiz options in seg18 are OK because they are the quoted choices.
- Decision: conversation.lines[0] en/ja, seg6 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa and slide item -> "Riko, would you like to start?" / リコ、はじめて くれる？ (A+B).

#### L506 Going-to Bingo
- A: The bingo game is natural ("Are you feeling lucky tonight?", "Winners, all of you!"). The teacherScript "find someone who says yes" does not fit, because most boxes are What questions. seg19 "Are you, so answer, Yes, I am." is not a sentence.
- B: The same flag on the teacherScript. seg8 "What are you going to, for any plan." is unclear. "Yes, I do" is a good distractor.
- C: seg7 "Haru jumps. Yes! Bingo! Wow, you got a line!" sounds like Haru praising himself, because Riko's line has no speaker. seg8 and seg19 stack commas.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Ask your friends and mark each box." (A+B). seg7 add "Riko says," (EN) and リコちゃん (JA) (B+C). seg8 -> "Use What are you going to for any plan. Use Are you going to for yes or no." (A+C). seg19 -> "The question starts with Are you, so say, Yes, I am." (A+C).

#### L507 Weekend Plan Talk
- A: The weekend talk is natural ("Can I come, too? I won't be late!", "It's a deal!"). seg8 "How about you? passes the talk to your friend" is not idiomatic. "Why don't we, is" has a stray comma between subject and verb.
- B: "Why don't we" as an invitation and the "Why didn't we" distractor are well chosen. "Maybe next time!" for a busy friend is good.
- C: seg7 "... I won't be late! Of course!" sounds like Riko answering herself.
- Decision: seg7 add "Haru says," (EN) and ハルくん (JA) (B+C). seg8 -> "How about you? gives your friend a turn to talk. Why don't we is a friendly way to invite." (objective + A+C), JA はなす ばんを わたす.

#### L508 A Note to Future Me
- A: The time capsule and the notes are warm ("Are you a picture book artist now?", "Your future is a big adventure!"). seg8 "I will, for a promise to yourself. I hope, for a wish." is the same fragment problem. seg19 "It's A! You are." does not say why.
- B: The seg2 JA ほんの すこしの ぎょう is not natural. seg19 JA says you の あとは are, but the EN only says "You are." The privacy line is good.
- C: The seg8 fragments are hard to read. "Seal the envelope. Press, press." is fine.
- Decision: seg2 subtitleJa -> ほんの 2、3ぎょう。 (objective, JA). seg8 -> "Use I will for a promise to yourself. ... Use I hope for a wish." (A+C). seg19 -> "With you, we use are." (A+B).

#### L509 Starfall Observatory Review
- A: The goodbye star party is a good ending ("I'll miss the observatory.", "But Luka will call us. He promised."). seg8 "A plan you already decided, going to." is backwards, and "decided" needs "on".
- B: going to (plan) vs will (guess, offer, promise) is clear in the teacherScript. The seg8 wording blurs it. The "I think it is going" distractor is good.
- C: The seg8 fronted fragments are hard to read aloud. "See, like the sea." works.
- Decision: seg8 -> "Use going to for a plan you already made. Use will for a guess, or something you decide right now." (A+C).

#### L510 World 17 Grand Review
- A: The dome show, the World 1 callback and the echo cliffhanger work. "Next is World 18, Echo Valley!" fits the next world. seg1 "Show your plans and dreams" is not natural ("Share").
- B: The future-forms summary (going to / will / I'll / because) is clear. The seg9 sound recap "there like their, and or!" is hard to follow.
- C: seg9 is one long comma chain that ends in "and or!", which sounds like two conjunctions. "Now World 17." lacks a comma after "Now" (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg1 "Show" -> "Share" (A+B). seg9 -> "Shun, shun, cher. We'll is we will. There sounds like their. And roar says or!" with matching JA (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **"Here's the point" rules written as comma fragments** ("X, for Y." / "A plan ..., going to."): fixed in L503, L506, L508, L509 (and the comma in L507). The same template is likely in other worlds' seg8. Search for `, for ` inside seg8 say lines.
- **Retold dialogue with no speaker tag** in seg7: fixed in L506 and L507. Check other seg7 lines where the last quoted line belongs to a new speaker.
- **Wrap-up partner vs aiTask speaker**: L501 aiTask starts "Hi! I'm Ms. Emma." but seg20 says "Next, talk with Haru!" The same mismatch is in L489 and in every L511-L540 lesson, where the starter is Ms. Emma and the partner rotates Kiko/Birimo/Haru/Riko. Not changed here. If the conversation partner is really Ms. Emma, the fix is "Next, talk with me! わたしと おはなし してね." (and the JA). Decide it once for the whole block.
- **"Yesterday" in teacherScripts**: L502 is the only one in this group, and I changed it to "Last time". The mechanical pass for W17-18 did not include it, so scan L481-540.
- Kept on purpose: direct speech without quotes ("the stars said, Come up and sleep with us!"); L501 "before you and me"; L502 "give them a name ... call it"; L504 "Thank you, Luka."; L505 "looks at the people", "ブロック5 の まとめ"; L506 "three boxes in a row make a line"; L510 "Now World 17.", double "Next" in seg19; deliberate wrong quiz forms ("It look a guitar", "you is", "we going show").

### Run 9 review notes: L511-520 (world 18, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I am. Or no, I'm not." says, OK, US praise, "I say" narration, lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,"). There are 39 patches in 10 lessons. Every lesson has at least one change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The world 18 comparison lessons are clean and lively, so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) the wrap-up segment packs "You can X, and you can Y" into one 13-17-word sentence (8 lessons), split into two; (2) wrong or loose spelling and stress rules ("one vowel and one last letter? Double it!", "In English, the middle part is strong", "just add it"); (3) wording a teacher would not say ("Ask with which and better, A or B", "Both is OK", "about as heavy as twenty elephants, or more", "say the most popular"); (4) Japanese slips (どの 花が いちばん for "more beautiful", びっくりする 先生 for "surprising teacher", さむい ひと, うけるよ for "I can float"); (5) the deliberate wrong form spelled two ways (beautifuler / beautifuller). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The lessons are 210-225 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L511 Hello! Hello! Hello!
- A: The echo opening ("Hello... hello... hello!", "Echo, are we right? Right... right... right!") is fun and natural. seg8 "Put e r on a short word" sits next to "bigger" (doubled g), but the examples it gives (tall, loud) are right, so it is fine for a first lesson.
- B: One new pattern (～er than) with tall/long, which need no spelling change, is a good first step. The seg17 quiz slide shows "mountain / valley" rather than the choices (single flag). seg19 has a 17-word sentence.
- C: seg19 "You can compare two things with e r and than, and you can make a big echo." is too long to read in one breath.
- Decision: seg19 say/subtitleEn split into two sentences (A+C).

#### L512 Taller and Shorter
- A: The tree-measuring scene and Birimo flying to the treetops are natural. The script's "Stand up, please." sounds cold from a teacher to a child (style guide 4). TPR "Crouch down small." is a little odd ("crouch down low" is more usual), but it is a learning item and clear (single flag, kept).
- B: Same flag on "Stand up, please." The this/that near/far point and the e-ending spelling rule are clear. seg19 has a 13-word sentence.
- C: Reads well. seg19 runs long.
- Decision: teacherScript "Stand up, please." -> "Everyone, stand up!" (A+B). seg19 say/subtitleEn split (A+C).

#### L513 The Longest River
- A: The river walk and the "safest way" quiz are good and safety-minded. "This river is very, very long." is fine for storytelling (lint info only, kept).
- B: The doubling rule is stated wrong. seg9 says "A short word with one vowel and one last letter? Double it!", and the script says "one letter at the end". Every word has one last letter, and long or deep do not double. The tipJa already says 子音 1つ. The script sentence is also 15 words.
- C: seg9 reads fine aloud, but the rule is wrong.
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn -> "One short vowel and one consonant at the end? Double it!", with subtitleJa さいごに 子音が 1つ (objective, wrong rule). teacherScript -> "Big has a short vowel and one consonant at the end. So we double the g." (objective).

#### L514 Heavier Than an Elephant?
- A: The museum whale and Riko's joke ("Birimo is lighter than all of them!") work. seg18 "It's about as heavy as twenty elephants, or more!" hedges both ways ("about" and "or more"). "A big bear is heavier than two lions." is plausible for a large bear (kept). The script says "Valley Museum" and seg0 says "valley museum" (single flag).
- B: Same flag on seg18, and the JA だいたい おなじ … いじょう is muddled too. "heavier than twenty elephants" reuses the target pattern and is true (about 25 elephants). seg19 has a 13-word sentence.
- C: seg18 is hard to say with the right stress. seg3 "Whoo!" for blowing a feather may be read as "who" (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg18 say/subtitleEn -> "It's heavier than twenty elephants!", JA ぞう 20とうより おもいんだよ！ (A+B+C). seg19 split (A+C).

#### L515 Valley Record Book
- A: The record-card presentations and Birimo's "I'm the cutest friend in the valley!" are fun. seg9 "Old, older, just add it." does not say what to add.
- B: Same flag on "just add it" (the other three rules name the change). The "number one of all vs just two things" point is clear. seg19 has a 16-word sentence where "and use faster than" loses its subject.
- C: seg19 runs long. "A very, very long neck." is fine (lint info only, kept).
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn "just add it" -> "just add e r" (A+B). seg19 split, "And you can use faster than, too." (A+C).

#### L516 More Beautiful
- A: The flower field and Ms. Emma's "They're all beautiful! Each flower is special." are warm. The script writes the deliberate wrong form as "beautifuler", while seg8 and seg17 write "beautifuller".
- B: Kiko's "Which flower is more beautiful, Ms. Emma?" comes after Riko (blue) and Haru (yellow) disagree, so the comparative fits, but the JA どの 花が いちばん きれい is the superlative. seg7 subtitleJa repeats it. seg19 has a 16-word sentence.
- C: seg19 runs long. "kan-ga-ROO." starting lowercase is the stress notation and reads fine.
- Decision: teacherScript "beautifuler" -> "beautifuller" (objective, consistency). conversation.lines[3].ja and seg7 subtitleJa -> どっちの 花が きれい (objective, JA). seg19 split (A+C).

#### L517 The Most Popular Animal
- A: The zoo survey and Ms. Emma's snake vote are funny and natural. Birimo's "Who chose the snake?" after "chose pandas / koalas" is a little uneven, but it is fine as "the snake option" (single flag, kept).
- B: seg9 "In English, the middle part is strong." is a wrong general rule, and the same line says pineapple is strong at the start. The JA えいごでは has the same problem. The JA いちばん びっくりする 先生 means "the teacher who gets surprised most". "Surprising" is びっくりさせる.
- C: Reads well. "ba-NA-na. to-MA-to." stress marks are clear.
- Decision: seg9 say/subtitleEn -> "In these words, the middle part is strong.", JA この ことばは (objective, wrong rule). conversation.lines[6].ja and seg7 subtitleJa びっくりする -> びっくりさせる (objective, JA).

#### L518 Better and Best
- A: The orchard is natural ("They're crunchy.", "Twist and pull!"). "Sweet job" is a fruit pun and OK.
- B: good/better/best and "Never gooder!" are clear. chim-pan-ZEE is one accepted US stress (chim-PAN-zee is also common; kept). seg19 has a 16-word sentence.
- C: seg19 "You can say I like A better than B, and I like it the best, with a reason." runs two quoted patterns together, which is hard to follow aloud.
- Decision: seg19 split, "And you can say I like it the best, with a reason." (A+C).

#### L519 Which Do You Like Better?
- A: The dinner chat and Birimo's "I like soup better. Can I have more?" are charming. seg8 "Ask with which and better, A or B." is not a sentence a teacher would say. "Both is OK, too!" is ungrammatical.
- B: Same flags on seg8. Giving the full model question is clearer for a child. seg3 subtitleJa さむい ひと reads as "a cold person". seg19 has a 17-word sentence.
- C: seg8 stumbles at "with which and better". seg19 runs long.
- Decision: seg8 say/subtitleEn -> "Ask, Which do you like better, A or B? ... You can say both, too!" (A+B). The JA already matches. seg3 subtitleJa -> さむい 日と しろい ゆき (objective, JA). seg19 split (A+C).

#### L520 Group Ranking Survey
- A: The survey-and-ranking task is a good world review. The script's data (4/3/1) differs from the conversation's (3/1), but they are different groups (kept). TPR "Make a line." is OK ("Line up." is more common; it is a learning item, so kept). seg5 "Like the four of us!" is said by Ms. Emma about four friends (single flag, kept).
- B: Birimo's JA みて、うけるよ！ reads as slang ("that's hilarious") in hiragana, not "I can float". seg20 "say the most popular" is a fragment the child cannot reuse.
- C: seg20 reads choppily at "and say the most popular".
- Decision: conversation.lines[3].ja and seg7 subtitleJa -> みて、ぷかぷか うかぶよ！ (objective, JA). seg20 say/subtitleEn -> "You can take a survey and count the votes. And you can say which one is the most popular." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Long wrap-up sentences**: the closing segment template "You can X, and you can Y." went over 12 words in 8 of 10 lessons here (fixed by splitting). Scan the other world 17-18 groups for wrap-up `say` sentences over 12 words.
- **Spelling-rule wording**: watch for "one last letter" (should be "one consonant at the end") and "just add it" in the -er/-est and ing rule segments of nearby lessons (L521-540 review the same rules).
- **Stress rules stated as general facts** ("In English, the middle part is strong"): fixed in L517. Check later stress lessons for the same overstatement.
- **Deliberate wrong forms**: "beautifuller" vs "beautifuler" was mixed in L516. If the lint has a spelling allowlist, list only one form.
- **"Valley Museum" / "valley museum", "Valley Zoo" / "valley zoo"**: the scripts capitalize them and segments do not (L514, L517). Not changed. Pick one if you want consistency.
- **"very, very"** (L513, L515, L516): lint info only, fine in storytelling, kept.
- Kept on purpose: L511 seg17 quiz slide items; L512 "Crouch down small."; L514 "A big bear is heavier than two lions.", seg3 "Whoo!"; L517 "Who chose the snake?"; L518 chim-pan-ZEE; L520 "Make a line.", "Like the four of us!", the different survey numbers in the script and the conversation.

### Run 9 review notes: L521-530 (world 18, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide), testPoints. I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, OK, US praise, "I say" narration, lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First,", "speed challenge", L528 "keychain"). There are 48 patches in 9 lessons. L524 has no change. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The English in this block is clean, so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) the lint flag in L522 (a 14-word sentence), split at "but"; (2) dialogue retold in segments where a line has no speaker, so a character seems to answer their own question (L521 "Almost!", L525 "Ha ha, yes!", L529 Haru's "Because..."); (3) wrong or confusing rules (L527 "L E S S means without care", L527 subtitle "Full" for the one-L ending); (4) odd wording ("Say each one the way it means", "The echo is so small", "They're, because dogs and cats are many", "a happy talk", "Slow, and you watch"); (5) comma-spliced lines that are hard to read aloud (L522 seg9, L528 seg13); (6) Japanese (はねの け, かりうど for an owl, すきな for "favorite"); (7) one capital mid-sentence (L526 "And Wave"). Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. The touched lessons are 204-226 s long with 98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L521 As Big as a Bus
- A: The fossil dig is natural ("Grab your hats and shovels.", "That's huge!", "I'll be as careful as a scientist!"). In seg7, "Almost!" has no speaker, so it reads like Birimo answering his own question. seg10 "Say each one the way it means!" is not something a teacher says.
- B: The as ~ as pattern with is/was and the -ly phonics are clear, and EN/JA match. Same flag on seg10. seg15/seg19 write 歯 as は ("その は は"), which is a little hard to read, but school.expressions uses 歯 and this is a single flag.
- C: seg7 needs a speaker before "Almost!" so the listener can tell who is talking. seg10 trips the reader. The teacherScript "Add l, y" vs segments "L Y" is a small style difference.
- Decision: seg7 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa add "I say, Almost!" / わたし「ほぼね！」 (A+C). seg10 -> "Your turn. Act out each word with your voice!" (A+B+C). The JA いみの とおりに いってみて is kept.

#### L522 Not as Fast as a Horse
- A: The race is fun ("Me neither. But I'm faster than that turtle!", "I'm not as fast as a horse, but I can fly!"). The lint flag, conversation.lines[4] "A turtle is not as fast as a rabbit, but it never gives up." (14 words), is natural, but it is over the Stage 2 limit. seg9 "Safe keeps its E, then add L Y." joins a statement and a command with a comma. TPR "Get ready, set, go!" mixes "Ready, set, go!" and "Get ready, get set, go!" It is a learning item and is clear, so it is kept (single flag, reported).
- B: Splitting at "But" keeps the model sentence "A turtle is not as fast as a rabbit." (= school.expressions[1]) whole. That helps. In seg1, the spoken aside "not as は ほど ない" drops the 〜 slots, so it does not mean anything when heard.
- C: The split line reads better aloud, with a natural pause before "But it never gives up." Same flag on seg9 and on the seg1 aside.
- Decision: lines[4].en -> "Everyone has a special power. A turtle is not as fast as a rabbit. But it never gives up." with the JA split the same way, plus seg7 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa and seg7 slide.items[1] (objective, sentence length). seg1 aside -> "not as fast as は うまほど はやくない." (B+C). seg9 -> "Safe keeps its E. Then we add L Y." (A+C).

#### L523 Quickly and Quietly
- A: The whispering bird-hut scene is lovely ("Shh... hello, everyone. Whisper with me.", "Bye-bye, softly!"). "Speak softly, please. OK." is fine because "please" comes at the end and is gentle.
- B: Action + -ly word order and the y -> i rule are clear. seg2 subtitleJa はね、はねの け for "Wings, feathers" is not natural Japanese. The goalJa uses the kanji 観察小屋 (kept, because sceneJa and goalJa use kanji in other lessons too).
- C: Reads well. "Angrily. Grr! Luckily. Phew!" is fun to perform.
- Decision: seg2 subtitleJa -> つばさ、はね、ちいさな くちばし (objective, JA).

#### L524 Colorful Birds
- A: The bird comparison is natural ("That one is. Its feathers are longer, too."). seg5 "The color of rainy clouds" ("rain clouds" is the usual phrase) and seg0 "Today two new birds are here." are a little stiff, but both are understandable (single flags).
- B: The er/more contrast and "one = the same thing" are well explained. The "colorfuler" quiz distractor is deliberate. The ful + ly = two L's tip is correct.
- C: "F U L plus L Y. That makes two L's." reads clearly. Nothing trips.
- Decision: no change.

#### L525 Nature Detective Report
- A: The report day is natural ("Put on your thinking caps.", "Knock, knock, knock! Like this?"). In seg7, "Ha ha, yes!" has no speaker, so Birimo seems to answer himself (same as L521).
- B: The one of the ~est + plural point is clear, and the quiz (hunter / hunters) fits. The JA いちばん じょうずな かりうどの 1つ (expressions[0], lines[1], seg6, seg8, seg15) uses かりうど, an old word for a human hunter, and it clashes with the vocab gloss かりを する どうぶつ. expressions[2].ja わたしの すきな とり drops "favorite" (いちばん すきな).
- C: Same flag on seg7. "Quiet, quietly, just add it." is easy to say.
- Decision: かりうど -> かりが いちばん じょうずな どうぶつの 1つ in five JA fields (objective, JA). expressions[2].ja -> いちばん すきな (objective, JA). seg7 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa add "I say, Ha ha, yes!" / わたし「あはは、そうね！」 (A+C).

#### L526 The Unhappy Echo
- A: The lonely echo story is warm ("Don't worry, Echo! We'll be your friends!"). But "The echo is so small today" (lines[0], seg0 "That was so small", teacherScript) is not how an American describes a sound. You say it is quiet or faint. seg12 has "And Wave" with a capital mid-sentence.
- B: Same flag. "small" is a calque of こえが ちいさい. The aiTask starter already says "the echo sounds so quiet today". The sounds/looks contrast (ears / eyes) is clear, and the JA ちいさい is natural Japanese, so it is kept.
- C: "That was so quiet." reads as naturally as before. The capital W does not affect TTS, but it shows on screen.
- Decision: small -> quiet in lines[0].en, seg6 say/subtitleEn/slide.items[0], seg0 say/subtitleEn, and in the teacherScript ("very quiet today", "weak and quiet") (A+B). seg12 "And Wave" -> "And wave" (objective).

#### L527 Careful, Not Careless!
- A: The rope bridge is fun ("Don't look down. Look at me. You can do it!", "I didn't need the bridge. I can fly!"). seg2 "Slow, and you watch every step." does not hang together. seg19 "L E S S means without care." is wrong, because less means "without" and careless means "without care". seg8 "a not so good word" is a little clumsy (single flag, kept).
- B: The ful/less pair is clear. seg9 shows "Full, less." on screen, but the ending has one L (L526 seg9 says "Only one L at the end!"). seg19 contradicts seg9 and the JA less は「〜が ない」.
- C: seg2 is hard to phrase. "You go slowly and watch every step." flows. "Ful" reads the same as "full" aloud, so the audio does not change.
- Decision: seg2 -> "Careful. You go slowly and watch every step." (A+C). seg9 "Full, less." -> "Ful, less." (objective, spelling). seg19 -> "L E S S means without, so careless means without care." (objective, rule).

#### L528 I Think So, Too
- A: The souvenir choice is natural ("It's an echo bird!", "How about one keychain and one postcard?"). seg13 "I think so, too, nod your head. I don't think so, shake your head." is comma-spliced.
- B: Agreeing and disagreeing kindly with a reason is well modeled, and "Different ideas are OK." is a good message. seg1 subtitleEn uses "/" where say uses "Or" (this follows the block-wide quiz-style subtitle, so it is kept).
- C: seg13 runs four "so"s and commas together, so a listener cannot hear where each rule ends. A question form gives clear pauses.
- Decision: seg13 -> "I think so, too? Nod your head. I don't think so? Shake your head. ..." (A+C).

#### L529 Because It's Cuter
- A: The stuffed animal choice is natural ("Long ears are cool!", "You all gave great reasons!"). In seg7, Haru's answer "Because it's softer, too." has no speaker, so it reads like Riko answers her own question.
- B: I like ~ better because ~ and Why? / Because are clear. The "Yes, I do." distractor is good. seg3 "Sip, sip" vs JA ごくごく (gulp) is a slight mismatch (single flag, kept).
- C: Same flag on seg7. The rest reads well ("A playful rabbit, hop, hop!").
- Decision: seg7 say/subtitleEn/subtitleJa add "Haru says," / ハルくん (A+C).

#### L530 Dogs or Cats?
- A: The talk circle is natural ("Who wants to start?", "Dogs are more playful, but cats are more relaxing."). seg8 "They're, because dogs and cats are many." is not English. seg19 "Kind words make a happy talk." is not idiomatic.
- B: Same flag on seg8. The teacherScript already has the clear version ("We say they because we mean all dogs."). The "no pet needed" note is good.
- C: seg8 trips on "They're, because". "a happy discussion" reads fine and matches the lesson word and the JA はなしあい.
- Decision: seg8 -> "We say they're because we mean all dogs or all cats." (A+B). seg19 -> "Kind words make a happy discussion." (A+C).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Retold dialogue with no speaker** (seg6/seg7 "Talk" segments): when Ms. Emma's own reply or a second speaker's answer follows a question with no "I say," / "X says,", the listener hears the asker answering themselves. Fixed in L521, L525 and L529. Scan other lessons for "? [A-Z][a-z]+!" right after "asks, ...?" or "Like this?" with no speaker tag.
- **Wrap-up lines "You can say X and Y"** where X and Y are bare phrases (L522 "not as fast as and faster than", L526, L528 "agree with I think so, too, and say I don't think so kindly", L529 "I like this one better because, and answer why"): understandable, but hard to read aloud. Not changed here (single flags, the same template in every lesson). If you want one fix, split them into two sentences ("You can say X. You can say Y, too.").
- **Spoken JA asides in seg1** that drop the 〜 slots ("not as は ほど ない", L527 "Don't be で しないで"): fixed in L522 only. L527's aside still makes sense, so it is kept.
- **"ful" vs "full" on screen**: L527 seg9 showed "Full" for the suffix. Check other -ful lessons for "Full" used as the ending name.
- **teacherScript "l, y" vs segments "L Y"** for letter spell-outs: a style mix (L521-L524). Not changed.
- **TPR "Get ready, set, go!"** (L522 tpr[0]): kept, because it is a learning item. "Ready, set, go!" would be more standard, but it would need an ID change across tpr, seg11, seg13 and the slides.
- Kept on purpose: "colorfuler", "It's one of the best hunter." and "Walk loudly." (quiz distractors); L523 "Speak softly, please." (please at the end is gentle); L524 "rainy clouds"; L527 "Someone may fall!"; L527 seg8 "a not so good word"; L528 seg1 subtitleEn "/"; L529 "Sip, sip" / ごくごく; L530 Birimo's "I think so, too. And I like birds the best!".

### Run 9 review notes: L531-540 (world 18, Stage 2)

These notes cover school expressions and grammar, vocab, TPR, phonics words and tips, conversation lines and aiTask, recap, teacherScript, and every teacher segment (say, subtitleJa, subtitleEn, slide). I did not redo or undo the block-wide fixes (double end marks, quiz "A / B" subtitles, "Yes, I am. Or no, I'm not." says, OK, US praise, "I say", lowercase after "Now, / Then, / Next, / First, / After that, / Finally,", "speed challenge"). There are 37 patches in 10 lessons. Every lesson has at least one change. L537 has only one small fix. There are no learning-item ID changes and no title changes. The comparison English in this world is clean and well sequenced, so most fixes are small. Themes: (1) wrap-up lines that join two skills with ", and" into 15-17 words, over the Stage 2 limit (L531, L536, L538, L540 seg0); (2) word-for-word glosses a native speaker would not say ("more far", "the bag of Riko", "in all time", "Tell the differences", "Put on your magnifying glasses"); (3) logic slips (the echo saying "Our echo is bigger than ever!", "Every choice is OK" after a two-way question, a bare "Because ..." line); (4) TTS problems ("like fl", "I say it, you are the echo", lowercase quoted model answers); (5) one JA line that dropped "louder". Every new English sentence has 12 words or fewer. The check script passes: ALL OK. All ten lessons are 208-218 s long with 97-98% English, and every slide item is valid.

#### L531 Animal Superpowers
- A: The quiz show with the little frog is fun ("A frog with no flies is an unhappy frog!", "Faster than a cheetah!"). The teacherScript gloss "Farther means more far." teaches the wrong form ("more far") that the lesson is trying to replace. The facts check out (penguins swim faster than ducks, kangaroos can clear a car).
- B: Same flag on "more far" (L314 fixed "more tall" for the same reason). The un = opposite rule is clear. The seg19 wrap-up is 17 words.
- C: Reads well. "Boing, boing!" and "Ribbit, ribbit!" are easy. The seg19 sentence is too long for one breath.
- Decision: teacherScript "Farther means more far." -> "Farther means a longer way." (A+B). seg19 split into "... with can, er, and than. And you know un words." (A+C).

#### L532 Which Is Older?
- A: The old-town tour is natural ("Grab your hats.", "I'll fly up and check."). In the teacherScript, the model answers are written lowercase ("say, the tower is. Or, the tower is older."), unlike every other quoted line in the product. "Say just the winner" for the older thing is playful and fine.
- B: The short-answer rule (The tower is.) and the one-tower-so-is quiz are well chosen. "Un can also mean undo" is a clear second meaning of un-.
- C: "Or, the tower is older." reads aloud as Ms. Emma's own statement, not a model answer.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "To answer, say, The tower is. Or say, The tower is older." (A+C).

#### L533 Compare Two Pictures
- A: The spot-the-difference game is natural ("Maybe it was you, Birimo!", "Sharp eyes, detective!"). seg0 "Put on your magnifying glasses." is wrong (you hold one, and the JA says もって). seg0 "Ms. Emma has two pictures" has her talk about herself in the third person. seg1 "Tell the differences." sounds like the idiom "tell the difference" (= tell apart).
- B: Same flag on seg1 (JA ちがいを つたえよう = talk about them) and on seg0 (EN/JA mismatch). "untidy" is understood in the US, but "messy" is the everyday word (learning item, kept, reported).
- C: The third-person "Ms. Emma has" is odd in her own voice. The rest reads well.
- Decision: seg0 -> "Get out your magnifying glasses. I have two pictures of the valley." (A+B, A+C). The JA (エマ先生は) is natural Japanese teacher talk and stays. seg1 -> "Talk about the differences." (A+B).

#### L534 My Comparison Sentences
- A: The read-aloud circle and Birimo's cookie joke are fun. "Riko's bag means the bag of Riko." (teacherScript, seg8) is a word-for-word gloss of リコの かばん that no English speaker uses. Kiko's "Because you always choose the biggest one" has no why-question before it. A child would say "That's because ...".
- B: Same flags on "the bag of Riko" and the bare "Because" fragment, which is a known learner error and a bad model. seg2 "from a big letter to a dot" uses kid words, but this is a Stage 2 writing lesson, and the JA says ピリオド. "capital letter" and "period" are the school terms (as in L448). "Compare things, not people" is a good kindness note.
- C: "use 's" in seg19 may be read as a bare "s", but it is clear with the slide (single flag, kept).
- Decision: teacherScript and seg8 "the bag of Riko" -> "the bag that belongs to Riko" (A+B). seg2 -> "from a capital letter to a period" (A+B). conversation.lines[5], seg7 say/subtitleEn and seg7 slide.items[1] "Because you ..." -> "That's because you ..." (A+B). The JA ...えらぶからね already fits. No tests reference these lines.

#### L535 Echo Valley Quiz Show
- A: The quiz show is natural ("I'm your host, Ms. Emma.", "Beep!", "Unbelievable! You're all champions!"). The facts are right (giraffe, Jupiter, cheetah, blue whale). seg19 "with the est" drops the "and" that seg1 uses ("the and est").
- B: Same flag on seg19 (the JA says the と est). "Speed round!" in seg13 differs from "Speed challenge!", but it fits a quiz show (kept on purpose).
- C: "ask quiz questions with the est and answer with It's" runs two skills together and "the est" sounds like one strange word.
- Decision: seg19 -> "You can make quiz questions with the and est. And you can answer with It's." (A+C).

#### L536 The Biggest Echo
- A: The planning meeting is warm ("Let's shout kind words, not just hello."). The teacherScript gloss "Ever means in all time." is not idiomatic. The fixed phrase is "of all time". seg14 "I say it, you are the echo." is a comma splice.
- B: Same flag on "in all time". The Let's (strong) vs Why don't we (softer) point is a good, simple contrast. The seg18 JA こだまが どんどん げんきに なってる drops "louder" (the conversation JA has おおきく、げんきに). The seg19 wrap-up is 16 words.
- C: seg14 sounds clipped aloud. seg19 is too long for one breath. "Hello? ... hello..." works well as an echo.
- Decision: teacherScript -> "Ever means of all time." (A+B). seg14 -> "I say it, and you're the echo." (A+C). seg18 subtitleJa -> どんどん おおきく、げんきに なってる (objective, JA). seg19 split into "... with Let's and Why don't we. And you made the biggest echo ever." (A+C).

#### L537 Thank You, Echo
- A: The thank-you scene is natural ("Goodbye, Echo! You're the best!", "Pinky promise!"). "We'll come back again." is a little redundant, but it is very common speech and a learning item (kept). seg9 "Ful sounds soft, like fl." is not a model a teacher gives.
- B: Same flag on seg9. The JA (ful は「フル」より かるく) means "lighter than フル", not "like fl". Thank you for + ing and the "for play" quiz are well chosen.
- C: TTS may spell out "fl" or read it as a strange syllable. "Very, very softly." for a whisper is natural teacher talk (kept).
- Decision: seg9 "Ful sounds soft, like fl." -> "Say ful short and soft." (A+B+C).

#### L538 Valley Guidebook
- A: The guidebook project is natural ("Hello, travel writers!", "Say cheese! Click!", "It's quieter in the morning, so come early!"). "You don't need to write the real name." is a good privacy note.
- B: The short word -est / long word most rule and the "beautifulest" quiz are clear. seg8 subtitleJa repeats ながい ことばは most twice, which follows the say aside (harmless, kept). vocab visitor JA おとずれる ひと is a little stiff (single flag, kept). The seg19 wrap-up is 15 words.
- C: The stress spell-outs (vol-CA-no, CAM-er-a) follow the pattern used in earlier worlds. seg19 is too long for one breath.
- Decision: seg19 split into "... the most beautiful place. And you can say what visitors can do there." (A+C).

#### L539 Echo Valley Review
- A: The goodbye hike is natural ("Step, step, puff, puff!", "This is the best lunch ever!"). "This mountain is the tallest" is fine for kids ("highest" is also common). seg8 "Dogs or cats? Every choice is OK!" is unnatural after a two-way question.
- B: Same flag on seg8 (the JA どれを えらんでも also implies more than two). The er/est, more/most, good/better/best and as ~ as summary is clear. seg3 JA しずかな たくさんの みず is a little clumsy but understandable (single flag, kept).
- C: Reads well. seg18 "Perfect! I think so, too!" calls back to the conversation. It is fine aloud (single flag, kept).
- Decision: seg8 "Every choice is OK!" -> "Either one is OK!", JA -> どっちを えらんでも オーケー！ (A+B).

#### L540 World 18 Grand Review
- A: The comparison show, the World 1 TPR callback and the "clink, clank" cliffhanger into World 19 work well. In seg6, "And the echo answers, Our echo is bigger than ever!" has the echo call itself "our echo". In the teacherScript this is everyone's shout.
- B: Same flag on seg6 (JA こだま「わたしたちの こだまは…」 has the same problem). The because-reason goal is a good final step. The seg0 opening is a 15-word three-part sentence.
- C: seg0 is too long for one breath. The seg18 list "er, est, more, most, better, best, and as fast as" is 14 words, but it is a list with natural pauses and matches the slide (kept).
- Decision: seg0 split into "The valley stage is ready. The animals are in the audience, and the echo is cheering." (A+C). seg6 say/subtitleEn "And the echo answers," -> "And we all shout," with JA こだま「 -> みんな「 (A+B).

#### Block-wide notes
- **Wrap-up lines joining two skills with ", and you ..."** (L531, L536, L538) go over 12 words. I split them with "And you ...", the same fix as L421, L423 and L430. Check the last segment of every lesson for this template.
- **Word-for-word glosses in "X means ~" lines**: "more far" (L531), "the bag of Riko" (L534), "in all time" (L536). Other worlds may have the same "～ means the ～ of ～" possessive gloss or "more + short adjective".
- **Lowercase quoted model answers in teacherScripts** ("say, the tower is." in L532). Elsewhere the product capitalizes quoted sentences after "say,". A scan for `say, [a-z]` in teacherScripts may find more.
- **Ms. Emma in the third person** in her own segments ("Ms. Emma has", L533 seg0). The block fix covered only "Ms. Emma says". A scan for "Ms. Emma (has|is|wants|likes)" inside teacher.segments say may find more.
- **"untidy"** (L533 phonics and vocab context) is understood in the US, but "messy" is the everyday word. It is a phonics learning item (un- + tidy), so I kept it and am reporting it.
- **TTS stress spell-outs** (vol-CA-no, CAM-er-a, AN-i-mal) are kept as in earlier worlds. If the TTS reads the capitals as letter names, add a lexicon hint.
- Kept on purpose: "big letter" in Stage 1 worlds (only L534, a Stage 2 writing lesson, was changed to "capital letter"); L535 "Speed round!" in the quiz show; L537 "We'll come back again." and "Very, very softly."; L539 "the tallest" for a mountain and "I think so, too!" after the quiz; L540 seg18 long skills list; L534 seg19 "use 's"; L538 vocab visitor おとずれる ひと.

Today, we use them for plans. Today's sentence is: First, we're going to eat. Then, we're going to watch the stars. Say it with me. First, we're going to eat. Then, we're going to watch the stars. | 486 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We're in the weather room of the observatory. There are lots of screens and maps. Stargazers need good weather. If it rains, we can't see the stars! Today, we learn a new word for the future, will. Today's sentence is: Will it rain tomorrow? Say it with me. Will it rain tomorrow? Great! The answer is, I think it will. Or, I don't think so. We aren't sure about tomorrow, so we say I think. わからない ことを よそうする ときは will を つかうよ。 Let's practice. Will it be sunny tomorrow? I think it will. Will it snow tomorrow? I don't think so. Good job! Now, phonics. Television. Vision. Decision. Division. Listen to the end. Zhun. We spell it s, i, o, n. Not t, i, o, n. Okay, will it rain tomorrow where you live? Let's guess! | Hello, everyone! We're in the weather room of the observatory. There are lots of screens and maps. Stargazers need good weather. If it rains, we can't see the stars! Today, we learn a new word for the future, will. Today's sentence is: Will it rain tomorrow? Say it with me. Will it rain tomorrow? Great! The answer is, I think it will. Or, I don't think so. We aren't sure about tomorrow, so we say I think. わからない ことを よそうする ときは will を つかうよ。 Let's practice. Will it be sunny tomorrow? I think it will. Will it snow tomorrow? I don't think so. Good job! Now, phonics. Television. Vision. Decision. Division. Listen to the end. Zhun. We spell it s, i, o, n. Not t, i, o, n. OK, will it rain tomorrow where you live? Let's guess! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 488 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We're getting ready for the star party. Kiko is worried. She thinks it will be cold on the roof. Let's check the thermometer. | 493 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today, we're in the space station room. There's a model of a real space station. Astronauts live there for months! How do they live? | 495 | `teacherScript` | Good evening, everyone! It's Space Quiz Night at the observatory! We have two teams. Red team and blue team. Today's sentence is: Which planet is the biggest? Say it with me. Which planet is the biggest? Great! The biggest means number one in size. いちばん おおきいは the biggest だよ。 To answer, say, I think it's Jupiter. If it's correct, the quiz master says, That's right! If not, Good try! The answer is Mars. Let's make our own quiz questions, too. Which planet is red? Which planet has rings? What goes around the Earth? Great questions! Listen carefully, and don't shout the answer. Wait for your turn. Now, phonics review. I'll. Won't. She'll. Rotation. Vision. You remember them all! Okay, are you ready? Raise your hand and answer! Good luck, everyone! | Good evening, everyone! It's Space Quiz Night at the observatory! We have two teams. Red team and blue team. | 496 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Look at these photos on the wall. They're astronauts. They are floating and smiling. Do you want to go to space someday? Today's sentence is: Someday I will go to space. Say it with me. Someday I will go to space. Wonderful! Someday means one day in the future. いつかの ゆめは Someday I will ～. で いえるよ。 I will be an astronaut. I will see the Earth from space. Your dream doesn't have to be about space. Someday I will be a baker. Someday I will swim in the sea. Let's practice. Someday I will travel. Great! Now, phonics. See and sea. They sound the same, but the meanings are different. See is with your eyes. Sea is the ocean. Meet and meat. Nice to meet you. I like meat. Okay, what's your dream? | Hello, everyone! | 497 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Today, let's use our imagination. Close your eyes. It's twenty years from now. You're a grown-up! What are you doing? Today's sentence is: What will you be in twenty years? Say it with me. What will you be in twenty years? Great! You can answer, I'll be a doctor. I'll be a chef. We aren't sure, so we can say, I think I'll be a teacher. Add a reason. I like helping people. I like cooking. りゆうを ひとこと いうと、もっと つたわるね。 Let's practice. What will you be in twenty years? I think I'll be a pilot. I like airplanes. Great! Now, phonics. Sun and son. One and won. The sun is hot. He is her son. I have one cat. We won the game! Same sounds, different spellings. | 498 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! We're in the Future Life room. Look! There's a robot folding laundry, and a model of a flying car. What will life be like in the future? | 504 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! Today, Birimo is going to play a boy who lives at the observatory. His name is Luka. Luka loves stars, and he wants to talk with us again. Today's sentence is: I'll call you tomorrow. I promise. Say it with me. I'll call you tomorrow. I promise. Great! I'll is also for promises. I'll write to you. I'll visit you again. やくそくの ときも I'll を つかうよ。 Your friend may say, Don't forget! Then you say, I won't! On a video call, you can ask, Can you hear me? Yes, I can hear you. Remember, ask a grown-up before you make a call. And don't share your address or password online. Now, phonics. Four. Pour. Your. Fourteen. The or sound is o, u, r here. | 505 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Welcome to the Future Fair! Everyone has a booth and a poster. You're going to talk about your future plans. Today's sentence is: In the future, I'm going to be a scientist. Say it with me. In the future, I'm going to be a scientist. Great! Then add more. I'm going to study the stars because they're beautiful. At the end, say, Thank you for listening. Any questions? さいごに しつもんを うけると、ほんものの はっぴょう みたいだね。 Listeners, ask one question. What are you going to study? Where are you going to work? A good speaker looks at the people and speaks slowly. Hold your poster up high so everyone can see it. And smile! Now, phonics review. Sport. | 506 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Let's play Going-to Bingo! Look at your bingo card. Each box has a question. Ask your friends and find someone who says yes. Today's sentence is: What are you going to eat tonight? Say it with me. What are you going to eat tonight? Great! The answer is, I'm going to eat curry and rice. Other questions are, What are you going to watch on TV? What are you going to do after dinner? Are you going to take a bath? カードの しつもんを どんどん きいてみよう。 When you get a line, shout, Bingo! Remember to say thank you after each answer. And your friends can say, Wow, you got a line! Now, phonics review. Vacation. Television. Picture. I'll. Tion, sion, ture, and the short form of I will. Okay, ready? Let's play bingo! | Hello, everyone! Let's play Going-to Bingo! Look at your bingo card. Each box has a question. Ask your friends and find someone who says yes. | 507 | `teacherScript` | Hi, everyone! The weekend is coming. Let's talk about our plans with friends. Today's sentence is: I'm going to go hiking. How about you? Say it with me. I'm going to go hiking. How about you? Great! After you share your plan, ask, How about you? If your friend has the same idea, say, Why don't we go together? That means, let's go together. いっしょに しようと さそう いいかただよ。 Your friend can say, Great idea! We'll have fun. If your friend is busy, it's okay. Just say, Maybe next time! Let's practice. I'm going to go to the park. How about you? I'm going to go to the park, too! Why don't we go together? Great idea! Now, phonics review. We'll. Won't. Sun. Son. Okay, what are you going to do this weekend? Let's make plans! | Hi, everyone! The weekend is coming. Let's talk about our plans with friends. Today's sentence is: I'm going to go hiking. How about you? Say it with me. I'm going to go hiking. How about you? Great! After you share your plan, ask, How about you? If your friend has the same idea, say, Why don't we go together? I'm going to go to the park. How about you? I'm going to go to the park, too! Why don't we go together? Great idea! Now, phonics review. We'll. Won't. Sun. Son. OK, what are you going to do this weekend? Let's make plans! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 508 | `teacherScript` | Hello, everyone! Today, we're going to write a note to ourselves. Not for today, for the future! We'll put the notes in the observatory time capsule. Today's sentence is: Dear future me, I will keep studying English. Say it with me. Dear future me, I will keep studying English. Wonderful! Write three or four sentences. I will practice the piano. I hope you are happy. Are you still drawing pictures? みらいの じぶんに しつもんを しても いいよ。 And remember, you don't need to write your real name or address. You can sign it, From me. Read your note to a friend. Your friend can say, That's a nice note! Maybe you'll open the time capsule when you're grown up. Now, phonics review. Future. Adventure. Explore. Before. Okay, take out your pencils, and let's write to future you! | Hello, everyone! Today, we're going to write a note to ourselves. Not for today, for the future! | 509 | `teacherScript` | Good evening, everyone! This is our last night at the observatory. Let's have a goodbye stargazing party and look back at World 17. Today's sentence is: Will it be clear tonight? I think it will. Say it with me. Will it be clear tonight? I think it will. Great! Let's remember. Going to is for plans. I'm going to visit my grandma. Will is for guesses. It will rain. Will is also for offers and promises. I'll help you. I'll call you. よてい、よそう、もうしで、やくそく。ぜんぶ いえるね。 And you can say, I'll miss the observatory. That means you will feel sad because you're leaving. We had so many good times here. Thank you, observatory! Now, phonics review. Station. It'll. See. North. Four. Okay, let's look at the stars one more time! | Good evening, everyone! This is our last night at the observatory. Let's have a goodbye stargazing party and look back at World 17. Today's sentence is: Will it be clear tonight? I think it will. Say it with me. Will it be clear tonight? I think it will. Great! Let's remember. Going to is for plans. I'm going to visit my grandma. Will is for guesses. It will rain. Will is also for offers and promises. I'll help you. I'll call you. よてい、よそう、もうしで、やくそく。ぜんぶ いえるね。 And you can say, I'll miss the observatory. That means you will feel sad because you're leaving. We had so many good times here. Thank you, observatory! Now, phonics review. Station. It'll. See. North. Four. OK, let's look at the stars one more time! | objective+lint | canonical spelling "OK", not "Okay" (style guide; lint canonical) |

| 510 | `teacherScript` | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 17. ワールド17 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed TPR! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Blast off! Float! Make a wish! Great! Now, it's the World 17 star show under the dome. Today's sentence is: Someday we will travel to the stars! Say it with me. Someday we will travel to the stars! Wonderful! In this world, you learned to talk about the future. Plans with going to. Guesses with will. Offers and promises with I'll. And reasons with because. Now, the last phonics review. Constellation. Mission. Future. We'll. There. Roar. Amazing! And look through the telescope. There's a green valley between the mountains. Hello! Hello! Hello! Our voices come back. It's an echo! Is that our next stop? But first, your star show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | Congratulations, everyone! This is the last lesson of World 17. ワールド17 さいごの レッスンだよ。 First, speed challenge! Look! Stop! Come here! Go! Blast off! Float! Make a wish! Great! Now, it's the World 17 star show under the dome. Today's sentence is: Someday we will travel to the stars! Say it with me. Someday we will travel to the stars! Wonderful! In this world, you learned to talk about the future. Plans with going to. Guesses with will. Offers and promises with I'll. And reasons with because. Now, the last phonics review. Constellation. Mission. Future. We'll. There. Roar. Amazing! And look through the telescope. There's a green valley between the mountains. Hello! Hello! Hello! Our voices come back. It's an echo! Is that our next stop? But first, your star show. Speak loudly and smile. You can do it! | B+C | kids do not know the term "TPR"; the say lines already use "speed challenge" (same as W13-16) |
